 Statistical Data Tables for Fish & Wildlife Service Lands (as of 9/30/2024) 
Category Definitions 
National Wildlife Refuges (NWRs) include the Refuge System lands, waters, and interests therein 
administered by the Service as wildlife refuges, wildlife ranges, wildlife management areas, game 
preserves, and conservation areas.  The 146.7 million acres includes more than 54 million acres of 
submerged lands and waters in refuges within marine national monuments (see Table 3A). 
National Monument Areas outside National Wildlife Refuge Boundaries are Refuge System lands and 
submerged lands and waters within portions of seven national monuments that are outside refuge 
boundaries. Pursuant to Presidential Proclamations 8031, 8112, 8327, 8335, 8336, 8337, 9173, 9478, and 
9496, and Secretary’s Order 3284, as amended, the Service manages or co-manages these areas as part 
of the Refuge System. See Table 3A and the Report Notes for more information. 
Waterfowl Production Areas (WPAs) are Refuge System lands comprised of small natural wetlands and 
grasslands that provide breeding, resting, and nesting habitat for millions of waterfowl, shorebirds, 
grassland birds, and other wildlife. Approximately 95 percent of WPA lands are located within the 
Prairie Pothole Region states of Iowa, Minnesota, Montana, North Dakota, and South Dakota. WPA 
lands are grouped by county and administered by one of the Service’s 38 wetland management districts. 
Coordination Areas are Refuge System lands managed by states under cooperative agreements between 
the Service and state fish and wildlife agencies.  
Administrative Sites are Service-managed maintenance facilities, offices, and off-site visitor centers that 
are not located on Refuge System lands. 
National Fish Hatcheries are National Fish Hatchery System lands and waters where fish are raised.  
National Monument Areas Administered Under Other Authority are lands in national monuments that the 
Service manages or co-manages pursuant to a Presidential Proclamation or a Secretary’s Order, which 
the Proclamation, Order, or an associated agreement with another federal agency excludes from the 
Refuge System (see Table 10). 
Table 1. Summary of All Service Lands 
Column Definitions 
Reserved from Public Domain refers to public lands the United States obtained title to through treaty, 
purchase, or annexation that have never left federal ownership.  These lands are considered “reserved” 
when they have been designated for a specific purpose by an Executive Order, Act of Congress, or 
Public Land Order.  Primary Jurisdiction refers to lands reserved from the public domain for the 
Service, typically for wildlife conservation purposes.  Secondary Jurisdiction refers to lands reserved 
from the public domain for another federal agency, for which the Service has some management 
responsibility (often called an “overlay”) through an interagency agreement. 
Co-managed Monument Areas refers to the portions of the Hanford Reach National Monument and the 
Papahanaumokuakea and Rose Atoll Marine National Monuments for which the Service shares 
management responsibility with the Department of Energy, Department of Defense, or National 
Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration (NOAA), and state or territorial partners (see Table 10). 
Acquired by Other Federal Agencies refers to lands and interests in land acquired by other federal 
agencies (e.g., lands purchased from private landowners by the U.S. military), for which the Service 
has some management responsibility.  Primary Jurisdiction refers to lands that were acquired by 
other federal agencies and transferred to the Service, typically to manage as wildlife habitat. . 
Secondary Jurisdiction refers to lands acquired by other federal agencies, for which the Service has 
some management responsibility (often called an “overlay”) through an interagency agreement. 
Purchased by FWS refers to lands and interests in land purchased by the Service.  
Donated refers to lands and interests in land donated to the Service. e. 
Agreement or Lease refers to agreements and leases the Service has entered into with other parties 
(e.g., landowners, states) to manage their land, or an interest in land, typically for a finite period.

 Table Footnotes
 ** Already counted in Table 3 - National Wildlife Refuges.
(A) The Aleutian Islands World War II National Monument includes 4,950 acres within the Alaska Maritime NWR.  Pursuant to Presidential Proclamation 8327, the National Park Service 
generally manages the monument, except that the Service manages the 4,950 acres of the monument within national wildlife refuge boundaries.  The remainder of the monument is managed by the 
National Park Service and therefore not shown in this table. In 2019, the John D. Dingell, Jr. Conservation, Management, and Recreation Act (P.L. 116-9) redesignated the World War II Valor in the 
Pacific National Monument into the Aleutian Islands World War II National Monument and the Tule Lake National Monument.
(B) The Tule Lake National Monument includes 1,354 acres within the Tule Lake NWR.  Pursuant to Presidential Proclamation 8327, the National Park Service generally manages the monument, 
except that the Service manages the 1,354 acres of the monument within national wildlife refuge boundaries.  The remainder of the monument is managed by the National Park Service and therefore 
not shown in this table. In 2019, the John D. Dingell, Jr. Conservation, Management, and Recreation Act (P.L. 116-9) redesignated the World War II Valor in the Pacific National Monument into the 
Aleutian Islands World War II National Monument and the Tule Lake National Monument.
(C)  The Hanford Reach National Monument in Washington includes the 161,486-acre Saddle Mountain NWR and an additional 32,965 acres outside the Refuge boundary.  The Service manages 
the monument under the authority of Presidential Proclamation 7319, and through agreement with the Department of Energy, which retains authority over certain monument lands not covered by a 
management agreement.  Only the portion of the monument within the Refuge System (Saddle Mountain NWR) is shown in this table.
35
[[page 36]]
Table 3A. NWRS-managed National Monument Areas and Associated National Wildlife Refuges - Acres by State/Territory and Unit
 Table Footnotes (continued)
 
(D) The Rose Atoll Marine National Monument consists of the emergent lands and submerged lands and waters of Rose Atoll in American Samoa that the Service manages in consultation with 
NOAA and the American Samoa government.  Pursuant to Presidential Proclamation 8337 and Secretary's Order 3284 (as amended), the Service manages 8,609,045 acres, including 1,613 acres as the 
Rose Atoll NWR, which pre-dated the establishment of the monument.  The Secretary's Order (as amended) emphasizes that the area outside of Rose Atoll NWR be co-managed and fishery-related 
activities will continue to be administered by NOAA as a National Marine Sanctuary.
(E) The Northeast Canyons and Seamounts Marine National Monument includes two distinct areas of submerged lands and waters in the Atlantic Ocean, one with three underwater canyons and 
one with four underwater mountains known as “seamounts.”  These areas are located about 130 miles east-southeast of Cape Cod, and include approximately 3,144,320 acres.  Pursuant to Presidential 
Proclamation 9496, the Service manages the monument as part of the Refuge System, in consultation with NOAA.
(F) The Marianas Trench Marine National Monument consists entirely of submerged lands and waters off-shore and adjacent to the Commonwealth of the Northern Mariana Islands (CNMI) and 
Guam, that the Service manages in consultation with NOAA and the CNMI.  Established by Presidential Proclamation 8335, the monument includes three units: the Islands Unit, the Volcanic Unit, 
and the Trench Unit.  Pursuant to Secretary’s Order 3284 (as amended), the Trench Unit and Volcanic Unit form the boundaries of the 50,532,102-acre Mariana Trench NWR and 46,779.50-acre 
Mariana Arc of Fire NWR, and include only the submerged lands and resources within these boundaries.  Pursuant to the Secretary’s Order Amendment No. 2, the Islands Unit, which includes the 
waters and submerged lands of the three northernmost Mariana Islands and encompasses 10,498,786.50 acres, is also a part of the Refuge System, subject to the terms of the November 29, 2016 
Patent transferring 112,499 acres of submerged lands to the government of the CNMI and the associated September 2017 agreement.
(G) The Pacific Remote Islands Marine National Monument is composed of approximately 313,941,850 acres of U.S. insular possessions in the Pacific Ocean.  Pursuant to Presidential 
Proclamations 8336 and 9173, and Secretary’s Order 3284 (as amended), the Service manages all of the monument as part of the Refuge System, in consultation with NOAA.  More than 1 million acres 
pre-date establishment of the monument, including portions of: Baker Island NWR, Howland Island NWR, Jarvis Island NWR, Johnston Atoll NWR, Kingman Reef NWR, and Palmyra Atoll NWR.
(H) The Papahanaumokuakea Marine National Monument encompasses 372,848,597 acres that the Service co-manages with NOAA, the State of Hawaii, and the Office of Hawaiian Affairs.  The 
Service manages 284,213,568 acres of marine, submerged, and emergent features as part of the Refuge System, including the monument expansion, Midway Atoll NWR, and Hawaiian Islands NWR.  
NOAA has primary management responsibility of the marine areas for the remaining 88,635,029 acres, and the Service manages the submerged lands as part of the Refuge System.

Table 10. National Monuments 
 
Table Footnotes 
 
* Monument acres administered by the Service under the 
authority of National Wildlife Refuge System Administration Act 
(16 U.S.C. 668dd-668ee, NWRSAA). 
 
** The Service has management responsibility for these 
monument areas pursuant to a Presidential Proclamation, but the 
Proclamation or an associated agreement with another federal 
agency excludes these areas from the NWRS.  NWRS staff 
manage these areas in consultation with the Department of 
Energy (see Table Notes). 
 
*** The Co-managed Areas column reflects the lands and 
submerged lands and waters for which the Service shares 
management responsibility with the Department of Energy, 
Department of Defense, or National Oceanic and Atmospheric 
Administration (NOAA), and state or territorial partners (see 
Table Notes). 
 
**** Includes privately owned, state owned, or other federally 
administered lands and submerged lands and waters. 
 
Table Abbreviations 
 
(AK) Alaska 
(AO) Atlantic Ocean 
(AS) American Samoa 
(CA) California 
(HI) Hawaii 
(PO) Pacific Ocean 
(WA) Washington 
 
Table Notes 
 
The Service manages lands and submerged lands and waters 
within eight national monuments, including five marine national 
monuments. Table 10 summarizes all monument lands and 
submerged lands and waters managed or co-managed by the 
Service under the NWRSAA and other authorities. 
 
The Aleutian Islands World War II National Monument includes 4,950 
acres within the Alaska Maritime NWR. Pursuant to Presidential 
Proclamation 8327, the National Park Service generally manages 
the monument, except that the Service manages the 4,950 acres of 
the monument within refuge boundaries.  Table 3 accounts for all 
acreage within Alaska Maritime NWR, including the monument 
acreage. In 2019, the John D. Dingell, Jr. Conservation, 
Management, and Recreation Act (P.L. 116-9) redesignated the 
World War II Valor in the Pacific National Monument into 
Aleutian Islands World War II National Monument and the Tule 
Lake National Monument. 
 
The Hanford Reach National Monument in Washington includes the 
161,486-acre Saddle Mountain NWR and an additional 32,965 
acres outside the Refuge boundary.  The Service manages the 
monument under the authority of Presidential Proclamation 7319, 
and through agreement with the Department of Energy, which 
retains authority over certain monument lands not covered by a 
management agreement. 
The Marianas Trench Marine National Monument consists entirely of 
submerged lands and waters off-shore and adjacent to the 
Commonwealth of the Northern Mariana Islands (CNMI) and 
Guam that the Service manages in consultation with NOAA and 
the CNMI.  Established by Presidential Proclamation 8335, the 
61,077,668-acre monument includes three units: the Islands Unit, 
the Volcanic Unit, and the Trench Unit.  Pursuant to Secretary’s 
Order 3284 (as amended), the Trench Unit and Volcanic Unit form 
the boundaries of the 50,532,102-acre Mariana Trench NWR and 
46,779.50-acre Mariana Arc of Fire NWR, respectively, and 
include only the submerged lands and waters within these 
boundaries.   
On December 21, 2016, the Department of the Interior (DOI) 
transferred 112,499 acres of submerged lands and associated 
mineral rights from the Islands Unit of the Monument to the 
CNMI via Patent Number 2016-1. DOI reserved a perpetual 
easement as part of the transfer, and entered into a Memorandum 
of Agreement (MOA) with the Department of Commerce and the 
CNMI for "Coordination of the Management of the Marianas 
Trench Marine National Monument's Submerged Lands Located 
Adjacent to the Islands of Farallon De Pajaras, Maug, and 
Asuncion."  The easement and MOU will ensure that the 
transferred submerged lands are managed and maintained as part 
of the Monument pursuant to Presidential Proclamations 8335 and 
9077. Subject to this transfer and MOA, Secretarial Order 3284 
was amended in September 2023 to include the Islands Unit as a 
unit of the National Wildlife Refuge System, which includes the 
waters and submerged lands of the three northernmost Mariana 
Islands and encompasses 10,498,786.50 acres.  
The Northeast Canyons and Seamounts Marine National Monument 
includes two distinct areas of submerged lands and waters in the 
Atlantic Ocean, one with three underwater canyons and one with 
four underwater mountains known as “seamounts.” These areas 
are located about 130 miles east-southeast of Cape Cod, and 
include approximately 3,144,320 acres.  Pursuant to Presidential 
Proclamation 9496, the Service manages the monument under the 
NWRSAA, in consultation with NOAA. 
 
The Pacific Remote Islands Marine National Monument is 
composed of approximately 313,941,850 acres of U.S. insular 
possessions in the Pacific Ocean.  Pursuant to Presidential 
Proclamations 8336 and 9173, and Secretary’s Order 3284 (as 
amended), the Service manages all of the monument under the 
NWRSAA, in consultation with NOAA.  About 1.25 million acres 
pre-date monument establishment, including the Baker Island, 
Howland Island, Jarvis Island, Johnston Atoll, Kingman Reef, 
and Palmyra Atoll NWRs. 
 
The Papahanaumokuakea Marine National Monument encompasses 
approximately 372,848,597 acres that the Service co-manages 
with NOAA, the State of Hawaii, and the Office of Hawaiian 
Affairs.  Pursuant to Presidential Proclamations 8031, 8112, and 
9478, the Service manages 284,213,568 acres of marine, 
submerged, and emergent features under the NWRSAA 
(including the monument expansion, Midway Atoll NWR, and 
Hawaiian Islands NWR).  NOAA has primary management 
responsibility for the marine areas in the remaining 88,635,029 
acres, and the Service manages the submerged lands under the 
NWRSAA.    
 
The Rose Atoll Marine National Monument consists of 8,609,045 
acres of lands and submerged lands and waters of Rose Atoll in 
American Samoa which the Service manages in consultation with 
NOAA and the American Samoa government.  Pursuant to 
Presidential Proclamation 8337 and Secretary's Order 3284 (as 
amended), the Service manages a total of 8,609,045 acres of lands 
and submerged lands, of which the Service manages 1,613 acres 
of the monument as the Rose Atoll NWR, which includes the 
lands and submerged lands and waters within the mean low 
water line of the seaward extent of the perimeter reef.   
The Tule Lake National Monument includes 1,354 acres within the 
Tule Lake NWR.  Pursuant to Presidential Proclamation 8327, the 
National Park Service generally manages the monument, except 
that the Service manages the 1,354 acres of the monument within 
refuge boundaries.  Table 3 accounts for all acreage within Tule 
Lake NWR, including the monument acreage. In 2019, the John D. 
Dingell, Jr. Conservation, Management, and Recreation Act (P.L. 
116-9) redesignated the World War II Valor in the Pacific National 
Monument into Aleutian Islands World War II National 
Monument and the Tule Lake National Monument.
 
[[page 62]]
62 
 
Report Notes 
 
Figures in our tables may show some changes from 
previous annual reports.  For example, decreases in 
acreage figures may reflect expired land leases or 
real property disposals made in land exchanges.  
An increase or decrease may be noted after new 
property surveys are completed or when additional 
information is entered into the database after the 
data has been transmitted from the Regions for 
publication.  Other changes result from corrections 
to previously entered data or data omissions. 
 
If you have questions about the information in the 
tables, please contact Allison McCluskey, Division 
of Realty, at allison_mccluskey@fws.gov. 
 
Table 1:  Table 1 summarizes Service-administered 
acres as of 9/30/2024. Secretary’s Order 3284 
Amendment No. 2, signed in September 2023, 
directed the Service to manage two Marine 
National Monument areas outside of national 
wildlife refuge boundaries under the authority of 
the National Wildlife Refuge System 
Administration Act (NWRSAA), when previously 
they had been administered under other authority. 
Accordingly, we removed Rose Atoll Marine 
National Monument and Marianas Trench Marine 
National Monument from the Table 1 subtotal for 
National Monument Areas Administered under 
Other Authority. These monuments are now 
entirely represented as a part of the Refuge 
System in the Table 1 subtotal for National 
Monument Areas outside National Wildlife Refuge 
Boundaries.  
 
Additionally, our count of National Monument 
Areas outside National Wildlife Refuge Boundaries 
now includes the Aleutian Islands World War II 
National Monument and the Tule Lake National 
Monument, which the Service manages entirely  
within the boundaries of Alaska Maritime NWR and 
Tule Lake NWR. The John D. Dingell, Jr. 
Conservation, Management, and Recreation Act of 
2019 (P.L. 116-9) redesignated the World War II 
Valor in the Pacific National Monument into these 
monuments. 
   
Table 1A:  Table 1A summarizes all Service-
administered acres by state or territory.   
 
Table 2:  Table 2 summarizes all Service acquisitions 
and disposals between 10/01/2023 and 9/30/2024.  
Negative acreages appear in Table 2 when the 
Service disposed of more acres than it acquired 
during the fiscal year. 
 
Tables 3-10:  Whereas Table 2 summarizes FY 2024 
acquisitions and disposals, Tables 3 through 10 
identify all acres managed by the Service. 
 
Table 3:  Table 3 summarizes national wildlife refuge 
acres by state or territory (note some refuges span 
multiple states).  
 
Table 3A:  Table 3A summarizes the acres the 
Service manages in national monuments under the 
authority of the NWRSAA.  The table includes the 
national wildlife refuge acres that are within 
monument boundaries as well as the additional 
NWRS-managed monument acres that are outside 
refuge boundaries.   
 
Table 4:  Table 4 summarizes Waterfowl Production 
Areas (WPA) acres.  WPAs are units of the Refuge 
System established under the authority of the 
Migratory Bird Hunting and Conservation Stamp 
Act.  The Director, U.S. Fish and Wildlife Service, 
and States must approve each county for WPA 
acquisition, and this report summarizes WPA 
acreage by county.  The total number of WPA units 
reflects the total number of approved counties with 
WPA acquisitions.  
 
Tables 3 and 4:  These tables include acreage 
transferred from the Department of 
Agriculture’s Farm Service Agency to the 
Service. When these transferred acres are within 
the approved acquisition boundary of a Refuge 
System unit (e.g., a refuge), we count them as 
part of the Refuge System unit.  However, when 
these transferred acres are located outside the 
approved acquisition boundaries of all Refuge 
System units, we summarize the acres under 
FSA entries, identified as “FSA Interest 
{State}” in Table 3 and “{County} FSA” in 
Table 4.   We include the acreage associated with 
these FSA entries in state and county acreage 
totals, but we do not count these FSA entries as 
units. 
 
Table 7:  The Ouray National Fish Hatchery in 
Utah is counted as a National Fish Hatchery 
(NFH), but it is located within Ouray NWR, and 
the acreage is included in Table 3 rather than 
Table 7. The Hagerman NFH in Idaho is 
managed by the Service, but the remainder of the 
land is managed by the state and appears in Table 
5 as the Hagerman Coordination Area.   
 
Table 8:  Table 8 identifies the Congressionally 
designated wilderness areas within national 
wildlife refuges and one national fish hatchery. 
 
Table 10:  Table 10 summarizes Service-managed 
areas within eight national monuments, including 
five marine national monuments.  The table 
includes a total of the monument acres the Service 
manages, separating acreage by the authority 
under which it is managed.
